ParlaMint-AT_2000-11-22-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00044_d7e4380	Ladies and gentlemen! In the time period since we last met in the National Council, the village of Kaprun and thus all of Austria have been affected by a terrible disaster. 155 people, men and women, elders and disciples, Austrians and relatives of other nations, were killed on a radiant winter day by a cable car disaster at the Kitzsteinhorn. All of Austria was shocked. The shock went far beyond the borders of our country, as proved by numerous condolence telegrams and also participation in the funeral ceremony in Salzburg. The Austrian National Council, which immediately after the disaster has cancelled or postponed a meeting, would also like to express its shock and its insignificance over this terrible disaster in a minutes of mourning and commemoration, but also its connection with the families and friends of the victims, regardless of which nationality and religion and which sex. It is an unhappiness that brings together, an event that keeps up, with the separation between people, as Cardinal Schönborn, together with the Protestant bishop Sturm, has rightly established in Salzburg. I would like to thank, from this point of view, all the helpers, in whatever function they have been in, regardless of whether their help has existed in a physical intervention or in support, comfort, assistance, or continues to exist. I should like to ask you, as members of the Austrian People's Office, to remember the victims of this disaster of Kaprun here in the House in silence. ParlaMint-AT_2000-11-22-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00044_d7e11705	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! High house! I will try to present in the few minutes available to me a serious change in the state real estate administration – probably the most serious change in the last decades, even last century in Austria. It is about moving the state administration of buildings and properties into modern management for just these buildings and properties. Thankfully, as my previous speaker has already mentioned, there have been the first seeing attempts in this direction with the BIG I in 1992, which has proved to be quite good, so that we can face this essential step today, privatize and separate it and in the future ensure market-compliant care – and thus also the new construction of needs – in future. Of course, there are exceptions; this too has already been mentioned. In the case of buildings for which there is no market, it is not useful to include these buildings, for example the historical buildings that are grouped together in the headquarters, or the buildings and properties of the federal army. The market for this is relatively limited – unless you want to get rid of the Neutrality Act in this way, so to speak. Or the judicial authorities: The market is also relatively limited for this. For the other real estates, there is a common management, an operating company and – starting with 31st time – an excellent preparation by the officials of the Ministry of Economic and Financial Affairs. January of this year – the transfer of ownership from the federal government to the BIG. The figures were also mentioned earlier: 33 billion Schilling – 30 billion are going into the budget, and 3 billion are used to increase the capital of the BIG itself. We are therefore on the solid side, because the underlying substance value assessments have resulted in a value of 90 to 100 billion schilling, so that a corresponding re-improvement within the meaning of the federal budget has also been agreed. In connection with this transfer, I would like to make an amendment to this report by Mr Firlinger and others. Since this is an eight-sided amendment, I ask Mr President, in accordance with § 53 para. 4 of the Rules of Procedure to approve the reproduction and distribution. The key points of this amendment are the transfer of properties and buildings, for budgetary reasons in a total of four tranches, so that in 2001 everything will be transferred to BIG's property. The last two tranches include those properties and properties that are already in the fruit pleasure. The second key point of this amendment is that unbuilt properties, which are already prescribed as road roads, will be transferred back to the property of the federal government without payment by the BIG. It was also asked why this amendment does not include the social plan for staff. To this end: It was introduced an hour or two ago in an own-initiative proposal for a Social Plan Act for contract staff and officials in the distributed areas and also the corresponding central offices, so that we do not need such a social plan in the BIG Act at the same time. The central point for us as delegates and as legislators is that with 1. In January 2001, when the BIG solution enters into force, the social plan for workers, contract staff and officials will also enter into force at the same time, so that the over 55-year-olds will have the opportunity to use a kind of early retirement model with 80 percent of the contributions. Overall, ladies and gentlemen, this solution, as I mentioned earlier, means a milestone towards a modern administration, a modern management of buildings and real estates. If you know that in the 1920s, the then international commissioner of the Republic of Austria had already rumbled, because it had such a split building and property management, then, I must say, the steps taken so far to standardize BIG solution 1 are certainly correct and today's legislative decision is a corresponding continuation and a milestone in the sense of modern building and property management for the 21st century. ==References== Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2000-11-22-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00044_d7e13744	Mr President. Mr. Federal Minister! High house! The energy consumption of federal buildings and the associated climate-related CO2 emissions cause an unestimated contribution to the impact of the climate. The CO2 savings potential in public buildings was estimated at around 250 000 tonnes of CO2 by the Kyoto Forum for the preparation of a national climate strategy. Now, in the course of the transfer of the federal buildings to the BIG, the federal government would have the possibility of anchoring a legal order to improve energy efficiency in the federal building stock. Such a procedure has already been developed by the Federal Government working groups under the coordination of the Federal Minister for Agriculture and the Environment in recent months. Nevertheless, the concerns of climate protection have not been adequately taken into account at the federal level. It is therefore necessary, to the extent that it seems economically sensible to make more energetic renovations in the building stock. This means that not only a climate contribution and a convenience gain for users can be achieved, but also an increase in value. Therefore, a clear legal order for the gradual and targeted energy efficiency increase of the entire building stock appears to be very useful through systematic energy renovations of those objects with the worst energy values. At least the Federal Government should be given the opportunity to order the implementation of climate change targets in the Federal Buildings sector in the future of the BIG. Due to long-term profitability, the concern about increasing rental costs is not justified, as current operating costs would be significantly reduced. In the case of flexible and target-oriented coordination between refinancing period and savings achieved, there would be no additional burden on the federal government. In the process of disseminating the federal real estates, it would be a great failure if the federal government did not recognise the possibility of commissioning the BIG to implement climate protection measures. Especially the credibility of the Republic of Austria with regard to climate protection vis-à-vis other countries, especially our eastern and EU candidate neighbours, would be significantly affected. We Socialists therefore support the Greens' request that the Federal Government be instructed to commit BIG in the area of the federal building stock to improve energy efficiency to the extent proposed by the Kyoto Forum working group. There are a number of measures such as setting energy and emission targets for the managed building stock. A predefined reduction target would be meaningful, a 10 percent reduction by 2005 and a 20 percent reduction by 2010. In the case of tenders, the best bidder principle should be extended by ecological criteria. Systematic building status surveys must be carried out, an energy accounting must be introduced, and by 2003 there should be "energy ID cards" for all federal buildings. There must be a series of priorities in terms of energy for rehabilitation. So there are many ways to improve the energy efficiency of the federal buildings. Let us take advantage of the possibility of environmental protection, the achievement of the climate change objectives, but also of a meaningful saving. If the Federal Government is always talking about saving: In terms of energy consumption and therefore also in the case of money wasting, it could be really useful and forward-looking. If you put on the red pen when you waste energy, you'll have us with you.

ParlaMint-AT_2000-11-22-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00044_d7e15210	Mr President. Federal Minister! High house! As a senior speaker of my group, I certainly do not have to give my consent to those points in the eight closely printed pages, which I, Mrs Reitsamer, will certainly not give my consent to, which directly concern the interests of older people, in particular those with 1. Pension adjustment to be made in January 2001. Let me say a few facts at the outset. First: Our pension system is based on the payment procedure. As much as I would be happy to allow pensioners to receive even higher pensions, I must be aware that any schilling that a pensioner receives or gets more must be pulled out of the pocket of an active person – whether it be in the form of his contribution payment directly, whether it be indirect about the state grant, i.e. from the tax pot. Secondly, For years, the number of years of pension contributions has fallen. The obvious attempts of the last SPÖ Social Ministers to bring up the de facto pension age by healthy people have failed. Thirdly, At the same time, however, the average pension period has increased dramatically. Each new retirement year is pleased to have a higher life expectancy than the year before it – a development that will continue in this form. Today, a more than 60-year-old comes to three Austrians aged between 15 and 60, and in 30 years there will be two. This development, if not countered, must necessarily lead to the collapse of our proven pension system. The only really goal-setting way to come to a recovery is that Mr and Mrs Österreicher must be ready in the future to use a correspondingly higher part of their life expectancy for the active period, so that the number of years of contribution payments and the period of pension relationship do not evolve further. In this respect, the Federal Government was on the right track by increasing the conditions for the provision of an early pension in several stages by 1.5 years, an increase which caused the angry protest of the opposition. In addition, however, a second path had to be taken, namely to establish a new system of pension adjustment, which at least ensures pensioners the maintenance of purchasing power. In this context, I would like to remind Genesis: A social law amendment law was discussed before the summer, initially in the pre-parliamentary area. The key points were: Firstly, the commitment to net adjustment, as the Minister of Social Affairs has already done, has been defined in the Hostasch period, according to a rather complicated formula. Secondly, If the inflation rate is higher than the net adjustment rate, the difference in net adjustment will be offset by a one-off payment. Thirdly, The decision in this respect does not fall within the scope of a Commission, as originally foreseen, but is taken in agreement with the Council of Senior Citizens. Now comes the crucial point – I don't see President Blecha anymore, he was up here on the gallery until recently –: In an internal discussion, Federal Minister Bartenstein, Federal Minister Sickl, President Blecha for the Pensionist Association, Knafl for the Pensioners' Federation, and Harring for the Seniors' Council – all five – agreed to this agreement. [[Abg. Dr. Khol: Blecha too?]] Including Blecha! Afterwards, the Council of Seniors, with the vote of Blecha, also adopted a unanimous decision to approve this new regulation. [[Abg. Dr. Khol: The "floh" has been hopped away!]] Now we are specifically coming to the first application of this law. According to the law, the formula per 1 is defined. January next year, a net adjustment of 0.8 percent, the inflation rate of the months of August to July – I will come back to it – is 1.5 percent, so 0.7 percent is offset by a single payment. The distribution of this 0.7 percent will be decided tomorrow in a discussion with the Minister of Social Affairs, in a discussion by the Council of Senior Citizens – again, including President Blecha. There are two schools of thought. One is that these 0.7 percent are to be paid as fixed one-off amount. At first glance, it seems that this is a socially just solution. However, there is also a lot to do with this: It is contrary to the principle of insurance that it would be very difficult at the lower end, because there would be the same amount of pension for pensioners, for example, second pensions for a high foreign pension. The other school of thought is that it's much easier to give this 0.7 percent as a fixed percentage, to upgrade all pensions by 1.5 percent, because the pensioners of higher pensions then pay back a higher percentage of this by their higher marginal tax rate. But as I said, This will be discussed in detail tomorrow with the Minister for Social Affairs. Now I come to speak to the famous inflation rate. ParlaMint-AT_2000-11-22-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00044_d7e17659	Mr President. Estimated ministers on the government bank! High house! The government coalition has decided to reorganise the Federal Building and Property Administration. The aim is to reorganise by economic and market principles. To this end, the Federal Building Administration is to be distributed and a real estate management company established. The breakdown is carried out in four tranches and is expected to be completed in 2001. It also regulates the rental conditions of the Federal Government and its disbursed administrative facilities, the transfer of property and the transfer of staff. This law continues the path that began in 1992 with the establishment of the Bundesimmobiliengesellschaft. The fragmentation of responsibilities is also eliminated. There are about 30 different departments which are responsible for the administration and care of the properties of the federal government. Finally, those cross-sectoral competences that allow legal space management of the federal government are defined, and there is at last also an incentive system for efficient space use for free-standing capacities. Ladies and gentlemen. This government template is based on a project objectively and objectively. It is another mosaic in our efforts to modernise Austria, its administration and its structures on the way to the third millennium. This was not an issue for the former governments with a ruling social democratic concept of state and economics. Cost-consciousness and an increase in efficiency, especially in the federal administration, play a major role for us liberals. I would therefore ask the opposition parties to accept this government proposal and the amendment proposal. - Thank you.

With us, I think, no one is thinking that the state can be the last, all-regulatory body to settle private conflicts at the end of the day. I think that the state, where private conflicts are, has to withdraw and intervene only if the child's well-being – as in many cases in divorces – is at risk, knowing that one cannot make final and final arrangements, provided that one does not want to completely undermine and regulate the private completely. So it said, for me very impressively, one of the family judges who had been heard as experts: The State should not intervene as long as possible in the matter of concern for the children, but only if the child welfare is at risk. And this is precisely what this draft is all about: that we come out of the present situation, that if people in a conflict-laden emotional situation, because they are going apart, yet are willing to advance the child's well-being and say, we will continue to be concerned together, the state must say to them: That's not going to happen, it's not planned. In the current legal situation, we are relying on conflict strengthening. The change now under way is the conversion – I hope the turnaround in the effects and quantitative figures, if we evaluate the law in one, two years in its applications – to reverse the trend towards conflict prevention. That is why it is absolutely right to say: Even in the case of a separation, as now, you continue to provide for the child's well-being, and only if this is not possible – for whatever reason – will, if necessary, support decisions and measures be taken with a judgment. We have outlined this bill, the Childhood Law Amendment Act in a very detailed, time-limited hearing with experts. This has already been mentioned; I stress it once again in order to prevent the formation of legends, here – for a change to the accusation of the zero reform – is once again "overrun". But I do not believe – and I have heard this many times in the debate – that an expert hearing can be won from one side or another, as it were, or would have to impose an opinion, but only decision-making aids will be provided to us. However, my understanding of this House is that we have to make decisions with the help of experts and expert opinions. That is why we are making this decision today in the direction of conflict prevention, in the direction of assistance in a difficult situation and in the direction of the concern to put the child's well-being in the foreground. It is an opportunity and an invitation I hope that as many parents as possible will accept it in this difficult situation.

ParlaMint-AT_2000-11-22-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00044_d7e24723	Mr President. Federal Minister! Dear ladies and gentlemen of the House! The amendment to the Xenophobia Act includes two changes. One has become necessary through a knowledge of the Constitutional Court, and this has already been debated in great detail from this point of view. The second amendment concerns the Asylum Advisory Council and the Integration Advisory Board at the Federal Ministry of the Interior. Both deal with individual cases, both are advisory bodies, both make recommendations, but both do not have any executive function. Asylum and integration are related materials – which will probably be granted to all those involved. Both areas have a common and important objective: to advise, assess and recommend specific and extremely problematic cases to the Minister of the Interior. One thing is in the foreground: to support the asylum seekers concerned, to help the person concerned. The person concerned, who is subjectively desperate due to his situation in life and who hopes in this support, does not care whether his concerns are dealt with in the Asylum Advisory Council or in the Integration Advisory Council. It is important for this person to be helped quickly in the event of his crisis. These advisory committees, as I have said by a member, have been set up as democratic valves in cases of doubt. I have also been confirmed that there have been case overlaps and parallels very often in the past. It was also the desire of interested parties to set up both bodies in order to do their work even more efficiently. Ladies and gentlemen! This innovation has the objective of simplifying administration, preventing overlaps and thus saving costs. I am not afraid of any loss of quality in the combination of the Asylum and Integration Advisory Board because the quality of the decision does not depend on the size of the institution, but on the material preparation and commitment of the group of persons making these decisions. As far as the quality of the decisions is concerned, I have very well understood another request from the Advisory Boards. Many votes on recommendations would have been very difficult in the past. Not only does a breach of the loading rules in any case affect the road safety of the respective truck, but also the road substance, the asphalt ceiling on the motorways, is heavily burdened. As the road load increases with the fourth power of the axle load, therefore an indirect impact on road safety – for example, by means of trackboards on the roads heavily driven by freight transport – will be felt for every driver, strict compliance with the tonnage limit on Austria's roads is essential. Every driver, every driver, will know the danger of the road safety being affected by the trackmills. Especially in the case of heavy rain, the possibility of causing an accident by aquaplaning in the trackboards driven by trucks into the asphalt deck is particularly high. This was also clearly seen by ex-trafficking minister Schmid, for after the announcement of the service instructions of the Tyrolean Gendarmerie, Minister Schmid – I quote – explained: The exceedance of the 40-ton limit – Minister of Transport Schmid has quoted this 40-tonne limit – must not be paid out for trucks. Minister Schmid was outraged by the service instructions to allow trucks to continue up to 45 tonnes. It was not enough only to show trucks above 40 tonnes and to pay administrative penalties, no, it also called for stricter controls on the tonnage limit. Obviously, due to his early resignation, he no longer came to implement a corresponding revision of the federal law. But he has signaled by his statement that he does not want to accept the current bill. In order to ensure that this breach is impossible in the future and that a clear legal interpretation is also enshrined in the federal law, we Socialists are proposing to amend the Federal Act, the Kraftfahrgesetz of 1967, according to the present proposal. The purpose of this amendment is to state clearly once and for all that a breach of the loading rules, an excess of 40 tonnes of total weight, constitutes in any case a threat to road safety. Given the burdens that the people, the environment and, last but not least, the road users have to bear, a clear law, strict control and a high level of punishment are essential. We ask you to support this new law, which will prevent any tonnage breach in the future. – Thank you very much.
